FINDINGS OF FACT 
 
Subsequent to a public hearing held on July 15, 2025, and after a review of the 
entire record, all pertinent plans and all testimony, the Worcester County Commissioners 
hereby adopt the findings of the Worcester County Planning Commission and also make 
the following additional findings of fact as the County Commissioners’ complete findings 
of fact pursuant to the provisions of Section ZS 1-113 of the Zoning and Subdivision 
Control Article of the Code of Public Local Laws of Worcester County, Maryland. 
 
Regarding the specifics of Rezoning Case No. 449: This case seeks to rezone 
petitioned area on Tax Map 26, Parcel 476, Lot C-1, consisting of 18.67 acres. The parcel 
is located on the easterly side of Greenridge Lane Road, West Ocean City, south of MD 
Route 707 (Old Bridge Road). The request is to reclassify the petitioned area from R-2 
Suburban Residential District to R-4 General Residential District. The petitioned area is 
currently improved with a single-family dwelling and large accessory residential storage 
building. 
 
Applicant’s testimony before the County Commissioners: Mr. Hugh Cropper, 
attorney for the applicant, explained the density differences between the R-2 District (4 
units per acre) and R-4 District (8 units per acre), as well as the manufactured home park 
density in the R-4 District (6 units per acre). The argument for rezoning is based on a 
change in the character of the neighborhood as outlined in the Planning Commission’s staff 
report, submitted into the record as Planning Commission’s Exhibit No. 1. He then 
provided a brief history of the original Greenridge trailer Park (identified as Phase 1) and 
Salt Life Park (Phase 2) which is currently under construction and contains amenities that 
serve both phases. The Planning Commission referred to the developments as “functionally 
integrated”. While acknowledging that a rezoning of land could result in any of the uses 
listed in the respective zoning district, Mr. Cropper stated that it is Mr. Odachowski’s 
intention to create a Phase 3 development, with an expansion of Salt Life Park onto the 
petitioned area. Access would be via the MD Route 707 (Old Bridge Road) entrance, 
ITEM 7
7 - 2

<!-- page 90 -->
2 
which he alleged was overdesigned to support the future phase. The development would be 
constructed with manufactured homes as a manufactured home park. 
 
Mr. Mark Odachowski, property owner, testified regarding the redevelopment of 
the Greenridge Road manufactured home park, and the development of Salt Life Park. The 
original exhibits presented to the Planning Commission included photographs of the 
amenity areas that were under construction; since then, they have been completed, and 
“updated” photographs were presented to the County Commissioners. Upon a question 
from Commissioner Bertino, Mr. Odachowski stated that the units were for sale, with 
ground rent charged. Within Phase 1, the purchase price is in the upper $100,000’s, and in 
Phase 2, the price is mid- to upper $200,000’s. Mr. Odachowski detailed the road and 
entrance improvements that were completed for Phase 2 that will support Phase 3. 
Commissioner Fiori expressed his support of the argument for change in the character of 
the neighborhood and appreciated the work Mr. Odachowski put into the entrance and road 
improvements. Commissioner Bertino inquired about whether the units were strictly for 
purchase or rent. Mr. Odachowski explained that the units could be purchased, and if 
rented, would only be allowed for year-round rentals, not short-term rentals. 
 
Mr. Frank Lynch, Jr., Maryland Registered Land Surveyor, had worked on the 
original Phase 1 redevelopment, as well as Phase 2. He concurred with the definition of the 
neighborhood, as well as the various changes that had occurred since the last 
comprehensive rezoning. Those changes included: 
• Resolution to several non-conformities in the original Greenridge Trailer 
Park via relocation of units and variances granted by the Board of Zoning 
Appeals. 
• Various sewer service area amendments to allow EDU’s to be purchased for 
the Phase 2 development. 
• Residential population growth in the defined neighborhood. 
• Four individual rezonings which are part of the Planning Commission’s 
record. 
• Development of the Sea Oaks Residential Planned Community, and the 
recent approval of the Crepe Myrtle Residential Planned Community on a 
nearby parcel. 
• The Land Use Map identifies the petitioned area as Existing Developed 
Area (EDA), which encourages infill development. 
 
 
In summary, Mr. Lynch concurred that the rezoning would be compatible with and 
serve as a natural transition for the Phase 3 development. 
ITEM 7
7 - 3

<!-- page 91 -->
3 
 
 
Mr. Chris McCabe, environmental consultant, testified that the Critical Area maps 
are proposed to be amended. This action is proposed to remove a significant portion of the 
petitioned area from the Critical Area. He reviewed the non-tidal wetlands on the site and 
obtained Maryland Department of the Environment (MDE) concurrence. There is a 
wooded area that will serve as a buffer from adjoining properties. 
 
 
Mr. Steve Engel, Registered Landscape Architect, Vista Design, Inc., concurred 
with excerpts that Mr. Cropper read from the Comprehensive Plan, both the 2006 and 
sections of the working draft plan, specifically the chapter on Housing. Mr. Cropper stated 
that in the working draft plan, the median age of Worcester County is identified as 50.6 
years, while the median age of the State of Maryland is 39.7 (U.S. Census Bureau, 
American Community Survey, 2022). He said that his client’s target audience is where the 
need is most. In the 2006 Comprehensive Plan, page 68, Mr. Cropper read the section 
entitled “Mobile Homes”, which encouraged improved construction materials and design, 
as well as the siting of additional mobile home parks with appropriate site design standards 
in order to expand the availability of this housing option. Mr. Engel concurred with the 
definition of the neighborhood and noted that he was involved in several of the changes 
cited. While not relevant for the rezoning application, Mr. Engel had prepared a site plan 
for the expansion of the manufactured home park with access via MD Route 707 (Old 
Bridge Road) and identifying the delineated non-tidal wetlands and associated buffers. Mr. 
Engel further concurred that this rezoning was consistent with the current Comprehensive 
Plan, as well as references in the working draft chapters of the future Comprehensive Plan. 
Commissioner Bertino inquired about emergency vehicle access and road design. The 
design would be reviewed and approved as part of the site plan review process for the 
development, if the rezoning was approved. 
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding the definition of the neighborhood: 
The County Commissioners concur with the applicant’s definition of the neighborhood as 
outlined in Applicant’s Exhibit No. 5. 
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding population change in the area: The 
County Commissioners agree with the Planning Commission and concluded that there has 
been population growth in the defined neighborhood with a strong demand for affordable 
housing, including manufactured units such as those in Salt Life Park. There has also been 
an increase in commercial growth, and there are sufficient services within a five-to-ten-
minute drive from the petitioned area. Since the last comprehensive rezoning, there have 
been four rezoning cases approved for additional or more intense commercial zoning in the 
defined neighborhood.    
ITEM 7
7 - 4

<!-- page 92 -->
4 
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding availability of public facilities: The 
County Commissioners agree with the Planning Commission and find that while this 
property is in the W-1/S-1 Category of the Water and Sewerage Plan, EDU’s are not 
available. However, this property is in the overlay service areas of Mystic Harbour and 
West Ocean City; therefore, it is possible that this property can get water from Mystic 
Habrbour and sewer from West Ocean City which could be purchased for transfer subject 
to Resolution 97-1. As Mr. Mitchell explained, the County is seeking methods to increase 
sewer capacity. In addition, Mr. Cropper’s written testimony states that there have been 
two expansions to the Mystic Harbour Sanitary Service Area along MD Route 707, 
permitting the additional influx of EDU’s into the community, which was not planned for 
at the time of the last comprehensive rezoning.  
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding present and future transportation 
patterns: Based upon the testimony presented, the County Commissioners find that the 
petitioned area fronts on Greenridge Lane Road, a County-owned and -maintained road. 
However, redevelopment of the petitioned area would be best served with access through 
Salt Life Park (Phase 2) to the existing road improvements and entrance on MD Route 707 
(Old Bridge Road). The County Commissioners and Planning Commission acknowledges 
that road upgrades have been completed or are in progress to MD Route 707 per MDOT 
SHA permit requirements. Upgrades include modifying Salt Life Park Phase 2 stormwater 
ponds to be able to capture the runoff from MD Route 707; widening MD Route 707 and 
adding a 500+ foot deceleration lane; increasing the pavement thickness to ten inches; 
adding a bike lane; adding sidewalks within the right-of-way. Based upon the testimony 
presented, the County Commissioners find that there will be no negative impact to the 
transportation patterns arising from the proposed rezoning of the petitioned area. 
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding compatibility with existing and 
proposed development and existing environmental conditions in the area, including having 
no adverse impact to waters included on the State’s impaired waters list or having an 
established total maximum daily load requirement: Based upon the Planning 
Commission’s findings and the testimony presented, the County Commissioners find that 
the petitioned area is adjacent to existing R-4 District zoning and the proposed housing is 
consistent with what the community needs. The Planning Commission acknowledges the 
redevelopment of the former Greenridge Trailer Park into Salt Life Park Phase 1, which 
was a park with a significant number of non-conforming units, many of which required 
variances. In addition, Mr. McCabe testified that Critical Area map updates are currently 
taking place for Worcester County, which will eliminate a substantial portion of the 
petitioned area from the Critical Area, leaving less than one-half in the Limited 
Development Area (LDA). Pertaining to existing wetlands, the applicant just re-delineated 
ITEM 7
7 - 5

<!-- page 93 -->
5 
the non-tidal wetlands line with Maryland Department of the Environment, resulting in a 
reduction in wetlands on the petitioned area and reduced environmental constraints on the 
petitioned area. Based upon the testimony presented, the County Commissioners find that 
the proposed rezoning of the petitioned area from R-2 Suburban Residential District to R-4 
General Residential District is compatible with the existing and proposed development and 
existing environmental conditions in the area. 
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding compatibility with the County’s 
Comprehensive Plan: The County Commissioners find that the property is designated as an 
Existing Developed Area (EDA) on the Land Use Map. There is a strong demand for this 
type of affordable housing in the neighborhood, and it is located near commercial service 
areas that also serve as employment centers. Infill development is a priority in the 
Comprehensive Plan and this development will provide additional housing while keeping 
the character and density of the neighborhood. The Planning Commission found that the 
rezoning of the petitioned area from R-2 Suburban Residential District to R-4 General 
Residential District would align with the Comprehensive Plan’s goals for smart growth, 
community consistency, and efficient use of existing infrastructure. Therefore the 
expansion of the manufactured home park is consistent with the Comprehensive Plan and 
in keeping with its goals and objectives.  
 
The County Commissioners’ findings regarding the recommendation of the 
Planning Commission: The County Commissioners find that the Planning Commission 
gave a favorable recommendation to the rezoning of the petitioned area from R-2 Suburban 
Residential District to R-4 General Residential District. Having made the above findings of 
fact, the County Commissioners concur with the recommendation of the Planning 
Commission and adopt its findings of fact. 
 
Decision of the County Commissioners: As a result of the testimony and evidence 
presented before the County Commissioners and the findings as set forth above, the 
County Commissioners find there has been a change in the character of the neighborhood, 
and that a rezoning of the petitioned area is appropriate. The applicant requests a zoning 
map amendment from R-2 Suburban Residential District to R-4 General Residential 
District based on a change in the character of the neighborhood since the last 
comprehensive rezoning on November 3, 2009. This change is demonstrated through 
substantial infrastructure improvements along MD Route 707, including road widening, a 
500+ foot deceleration lane, sidewalks, bike lanes, and upgraded stormwater facilities, all 
of which support higher-density residential development. The petitioned area is 
functionally integrated with Salt Life Park Phases 1 and 2 - formerly Greenridge Trailer 
Park - which has been revitalized through private investment in updated housing units and 
shared community amenities. The proposed Phase 3 infill development is consistent with 
ITEM 7
7 - 6

<!-- page 94 -->
6 
this pattern and aligns with recent land use trends, including recent rezonings in the 
defined neighborhood, the expansion of the Mystic Harbour Sanitary Service Area, and the 
reclassification of the Critical Area boundaries. The property is adjacent to existing R-4 
District zoning, the dwelling on the petitioned area is already connected to public utilities, 
and it is located within a service area that supports compact residential growth. The 
requested zoning conforms to the Worcester County Comprehensive Plan, which 
encourages infill within established communities while preserving neighborhood character. 
Given the compatibility of the proposed use, existing infrastructure capacity, and strong 
demand for affordable, cottage-style manufactured housing, a favorable recommendation 
for the rezoning is both appropriate and consistent with sound planning principles. Based 
upon their review, the County Commissioners conclude that a change in zoning would be 
more desirable in terms of the objectives of the Comprehensive Plan and hereby approve 
Rezoning Case No. 449 and thus rezone the petitioned area, Tax Map 26, Parcel 476, Lot 
C-1, from R-2 Suburban Residential District to R-4 General Residential District. 
 
Adopted as of July 15, 2025. Reduced to writing and signed _____________, 2025.

ZONING RECLASSIFICATION RESOLUTION NO. 25- 
A RESOLUTION OF THE COUNTY COMMISSIONERS OF WORCESTER COUNTY, 
MARYLAND, PURSUANT TO § ZS 1-113 OF THE ZONING AND SUBDIVISION 
CONTROL ARTICLE OF THE CODE OF PUBLIC LOCAL LAWS OF WORCESTER 
COUNTY, MARYLAND, CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION OF CERTAIN 
PARCEL OF LAND SHOWN ON TAX MAP 26 AS PARCEL 476, LOT C-1 FROM R-2 
SUBURBAN RESIDENTIAL DISTRICT TO R-4 GENERAL RESIDENTIAL DISTRICT. 
WHEREAS, pursuant to § ZS 1-113 of the Zoning and Subdivision Control Article of 
the Code of Public Local Laws of Worcester County, Maryland, Mark R. Odachowski, 
applicant, and Hugh Cropper, IV, applicant’s attorney, filed a petition for the rezoning of 
approximately 18.67 acres of land shown on Tax Map 26 as Parcel 476, Lot C-1 (“petitioned 
area”), located at 12254 Greenridge Lane Road, 850 feet south of Old Bridge Road (MD 
Route 707), requesting a change in zoning classification thereof from R-2 Suburban 
Residential District to R-4 General Residential District; and 
WHEREAS, the Worcester County Planning Commission gave the petition a 
favorable recommendation during its review on May 1, 2025; and 
WHEREAS, subsequent to a public hearing held on July 15, 2025, following due 
notice and all procedures as required by Sections ZS 1-113 and 1-114 of the Zoning and 
Subdivision Control Article of the Code of Public Local Laws of Worcester County, 
Maryland, the County Commissioners found that there has been a substantial change in the 
character of the neighborhood and the findings of fact relative to the criteria as required by 
law are incorporated by reference;  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the County Commissioners of 
Worcester County that the land petitioned by Mark R. Odachowski, applicant, and Hugh 
Cropper, IV, applicant’s attorney, and shown on Tax Map 26 as Parcel 476, Lot C-1 is hereby 
reclassified from R-2 Suburban Residential District to R-4 General Residential District. 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the effective date of this Resolution shall be nunc 
pro tunc, July 15, 2025.

enforcement officer shall be held personally liable under this Agreement and any 
extension or renewals thereof because of its enforcement or attempted enforcement 
provided they are acting within the course and scope of their employment or 
governmental duties and responsibilities.   
As a unit of the State of Maryland, MES is subject to the Maryland Annotated Code, 
State Finance and Procurement Article, Section 2-901, et seq., which prohibits certain 
provisions from being included in State contracts.  Accordingly, MES does not agree to or 
accept certain provisions in any contracts to which it is a party, including, but not limited 
to provisions which are prohibited in Maryland Annotated Code, State Finance and 
Procurement Article, Section 2-901.   
9.
TERMINATION FOR CONVENIENCE
The performance of work under this Agreement may be terminated by MES in 
accordance with this clause in whole, or from time to time in part at any time after thirty 
(30) calendar days’ notice to County.
10.
REIMBURSEMENT OF COSTS
The County shall reimburse MES for all costs incurred by MES in providing the 
services identified herein, including costs incurred by MES under the Contract between 
MES and MXI Environmental Services LLC, as detailed in the attached Scope of Work 
and Estimated Cost Proposal.  The County acknowledges and agrees that the Estimated 
Cost Proposal is only an estimate, based upon MES’s reasonable estimation of the 
quantity of HHW based upon unit pricing that may be delivered by the County residents. 
The actual costs incurred by MES may be more or less than the Estimated Cost Proposal.  
The full cost of a project, product, or service provided by MES to a Person, which 
includes business and governmental entities, is to be paid by the Person to MES, as 
required by §3-108(a)(1) of the Natural Resources Article of the Annotated Code of 
Maryland.  MES shall be fully authorized to incur additional costs under this Contract, 
provided that it obtains prior written approval from the Customer Contract Representative.

Appendix A 
 
Scope of Work 
Household Hazardous Waste Collection 
And Disposal 
 
Introduction 
 
Current Federal and State regulations dealing with management of hazardous waste 
exempt such wastes when they are generated by a household. Such wastes are referred to 
as Household Hazardous Waste, or HHW.  However, to better protect public health and 
the environment, many jurisdictions elect to separately collect and manage HHW to 
prevent their introduction into the municipal solid waste management facility.  To assist 
jurisdictions in the implementation of such separate HHW management programs, the 
Maryland Environmental Service (MES) has contracted for HHW management services 
through a fully qualified and experienced contractor, MXI Environmental Services LLC. 
Individual jurisdictions are able to secure the services of this HHW management 
company through MES.   
 
Objective 
 
MES has contracted for HHW collection and management services through MXI 
Environmental Services LLC, 26319 Old Trail Road, Abingdon, VA 24210.  The 
company will provide statewide service by accepting, classifying, and properly 
packaging HHW.  MXI Environmental Services LLC will also properly manifest, 
transport, and dispose of the collected HHW in accordance with all current federal and 
state requirements.   The responsibilities of MXI Environmental Services LLC are as set 
forth in the MES Service Agreement with the Contractor.  Under the provisions of that 
Agreement, MXI Environmental Services LLC shall: 
 
1. Provide sufficiently trained and qualified personnel for conducting an HHW collection event 
on a specified date at a designated location within a participating jurisdiction; 
2. Accept and properly classify HHW delivered to the collection site; 
3. Properly package, weigh, manifest, transport, and dispose of the collected HHW at a fully 
permitted treatment, storage, or disposal facility in accordance with the provisions of federal 
and state laws and regulations dealing with hazardous waste; 
4. Assess fees and unit costs for HHW collection services as stipulated in the Service 
Agreement; 
5. Provide documentation and reports to MES regarding the quantities of HHW collected, 
transported, and disposed; 
6. When requested, MXI Environmental Services LLC shall participate in meetings with 
representatives of the participating jurisdiction to organize and plan for the collection event. 
7. MXI Environmental Services LLC shall familiarize the local jurisdiction with the mobile 
collection unit, layout, and site setup requirements and operating procedures. 
 
ITEM 10
10 - 8

<!-- page 107 -->
MES Deliverables 
 
• MES shall coordinate with the participating jurisdiction and provide notice to MXI 
Environmental Services LLC of the participating jurisdiction and the date of the planned 
HHW collection event. 
• MES shall serve as liaison between the participating jurisdiction and MXI Environmental 
Services LLC. 
• MES shall be available to offer support to the participating jurisdiction in the planning and 
coordination of the HHW collection event.  
• MES shall supervise the HHW collection event and review all weigh records, manifest data, 
invoices, and other information developed as a result of the HHW collection event. 
• MES shall pay MXI Environmental Services LLC for conducting the collection event in 
accordance with the fees and other unit costs as set forth in the Service Agreement.  
• MES shall prepare and submit an invoice to the participating jurisdiction that shall include 
all costs associated with the HHW collection event. 
 
MXI Environmental Services LLC Deliverables 
 
MXI Environmental Services LLC shall provide the services as stipulated in the Service 
Agreement.  In summary, these duties include but are not limited to the following: 
   
• For each collection event, MXI Environmental Services LLC shall, within thirty (30) days of 
prior notice from MES, provide a mobile HHW collection unit for operation in a participating 
jurisdiction at a specified location within the State of Maryland.  MXI Environmental Services 
LLC shall provide the basic material and personnel necessary to: 
1. Safely unload, sort, consolidate, and package HHW delivered to the site; 
2. Respond to fires, spills, and/or other releases of HHW; 
3. Provide effective safety barriers to protect its personnel and participants from exposure 
to hazardous materials.  Such material components include, but are not limited to, trucks, 
tents, scales, tables, signs, traffic cones, barrier tape, drums, adsorbent material, labels, 
fire extinguishers, shovels, over-pack drums, plastic ground cloth, etc.; and 
4. Assist in traffic flow and safety.  
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall be responsible for professional quality, technical 
accuracy, timely completion, and coordination of all services.   
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall provide one point of contact to MES who will be 
familiar with the project and who will be on-site or available to be on-site during the 
collection event to provide on-site supervision during the event.  This supervision shall 
include, but is not limited to, overseeing removal of HHW from vehicles, interviewing 
participants on the characteristics of the waste delivered and the exempt status of the waste, 
and determining the appropriate handling and storage of the waste. 
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall properly secure and render inaccessible to the public 
all collected HHW not intended for recycling or reuse, including trash containers. 
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall provide on-site identification of all wastes received, 
and properly consolidate wastes. 
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall determine and record the weight of the collected 
materials before the materials are transported from the site for proper disposal.   
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall not accept any hazardous waste that is not HHW.  
ITEM 10
10 - 9

<!-- page 108 -->
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall comply with all applicable state and federal 
regulations dealing with manifesting, transporting, and disposing of hazardous waste. 
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall leave the collection site clean and environmentally 
safe and shall assume full responsibility for any repair for damages caused by its activities or 
those of its subcontractors. 
• MXI Environmental Services LLC shall provide MES with a copy of all hazardous waste 
manifests, weigh records, and other information developed during a collection event within 
fifteen (15) days of the completion of a collection event. 
 
Participating Jurisdiction Deliverables 
 
The participating jurisdiction shall: 
 
• Designate the date, time, and location of the planned HHW collection event and provide this 
information to MES at least sixty (60) days prior to the planned event to allow sufficient time 
for notification to MXI Environmental Services LLC; 
• Provide right of access to the site where the collection event will be held; 
• Provide sufficient support services to the collection event to accommodate the expected event 
participants;  
• Designate the types of materials that will or will not be accepted during the planned HHW 
collection event; 
• Provide facilities such as a roll-off container, pallets, or other containers to accommodate non-
hazardous waste, recyclable materials, or other acceptable waste materials delivered to the 
site during the collection event which are not to be disposed of by MXI Environmental 
Services LLC as HHW; and 
• Reimburse MES for all legitimate costs incurred by MES in providing the services as 
identified herein, including those costs as identified in the attached estimated cost proposal.

MEMORANDUM

Candace Savage, CGFM, Deputy Chief Administration Officer  
FROM: 
Dallas Baker Jr., P.E., Director 
DATE: 
July 29, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Assateague Point Water – Filter Replacement 
 
 
Public Works is requesting Commissioner approval to purchase three replacement water 
filters for the Assateague Point Water Treatment Plant. The total cost for the replacement 
equipment and labor is $58,965.00 (see attached quotation document).  Funding was included in 
the approved capital equipment budget for Assateague Point, account number 520.9010. Public 
Works is requesting to purchase the equipment from Peninsula Water Conditioning since they are 
the local sales rep for the Hellenbrand system which is needed to interface with the existing 
equipment. If the proposed equipment is not selected, significant modifications to other supporting 
systems will be needed at the treatment plant and require more funding than what was budgeted 
for in FY 26.  
The existing filters at Assateague Point Water Treatment Plant are 36 years old and original 
to the facility.  The existing filters are obsolete and no longer supported by the manufacturer. 
Without these filters being replaced the water plant will not function and the plant will not be able 
to meet its demand for water supply. 
Please let me know if there are any questions.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Nicholas W. Rice, Procurement Officer 
DATE: 
August 5, 2025 
RE: 
Request to Purchase – Landfill Litter Fences 
Solid Waste is requesting approval to purchase ten (10) Bull Litter Fences from Metta Technologies for use at the 
Central Site Landfill. The total cost for this purchase is $79,600. Pricing is based on a competitively solicited bid 
awarded to Metta Technologies by Salem County, NJ. 
Currently, the landfill operates with four litter fences that require equipment for repositioning. An additional three 
existing fences are no longer usable due to severe rust and age-related deterioration. The purchase of ten new 
fences will significantly expand our ability to contain litter near the working face of the landfill. This will reduce the 
area requiring manual litter collection and decrease the time staff must spend picking litter on Cell 5. 
Funding for this purchase, in the amount of $100,000, was approved in the FY26 operating budget under account 
680.7002.9010.070 – Capital Heavy Equipment. 
Should you have any questions, please feel free to contact me.

renewal price is $39,117.96 for another year of service.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Nicholas W. Rice, Procurement Officer 
DATE: 
August 5, 2025 
RE: 
Request to Award – West Ocean City Commercial Harbor Boat Slip Leases 
Recreation and Parks is seeking Commissioner approval to accept the eight proposed leases for the West Ocean 
City Commercial Harbor Boat Slips as outlined below.  Bids were due and opened on Thursday, July 3, 2025. Eleven 
bids were received.  The current leases are set to expire on September 22, 2025.  If approved the new leases will 
run through September 22, 2030.  The average value of slip increased from $5,328.25 in 2020 to $6,666.50 for 2025. 
All bids received currently occupy our slips.

<!-- page 129 -->
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Weston S. Young, Chief Administrative Officer 
DATE: 
July 29, 2025 
RE: 
Wicomico County Parking Request  
We have received a request from Wicomico County’s Office of the County Executive to park a vehicle at the county’s 
Isle of Wight Center, located at 13070 St. Martin’s Neck Road in Bishopville. The new Wicomico County Deputy 
Director of Administration lives in Worcester County and lacks adequate space to park the county-issued vehicle at 
his property. 
The Isle of Wight Center houses both the Health Department and the Treasurer’s Office. Outside of a renovation 
scheduled for this fall, staff believe that there is enough parking to accommodate an additional vehicle. 
If approved, the arrangement would allow a personal or Wicomico County vehicle to be parked on county property 
during work and non-work hours, respectively. Further, staff would request a basic waiver/indemnification 
agreement. 
ITEM 20
20 - 1

<!-- page 130 -->
Worcester County Sheriff's Office 
Matthew Crisafulli 
Sheriff 
July 24, 2025 
To: Worcester County Commissioners 
From: Sheriff Matthew Crisafulli 
RE: Axon Quote for System Consolidation and Renewal 
Nathaniel Passwaters 
Chief Deputy 
Please accept this request for approval of the accompanying Axon quote for the consolidation and renewal of our 
current body-worn camera and fleet camera systems, along with the inclusion of three existing Taser bundles. 
Pricing is based on Axon's cooperative contract with Sourcewell, contract #101223-AXN. This purchase was 
approved in the FY26 operating budget under account 100.1101.030.6110.050 (BWCs/Fleet Cameras) and 
100.1101.030.6110.190 (Tasers), in the amounts of $411,542.14 and 34,506.00, respectively. 
This proposed agreement streamlines and updates our existing contracts by removing non-essential items and 
incorporating critical components. Most notably, it secures 2025 pricing for the next five years, ensuring flat, 
predictable costs through the term of the agreement. 
The continued deployment of body-worn and fleet cameras remains essential to maintaining compliance with 
state-mandated requirements. While these mandates are unfunded, they play a vital role in promoting 
transparency and accountability. This consolidated contract allows us to meet these obligations without imposing 
any additional financial burden. 
The inclusion of the three existing Taser bundles further supports officer safety and enhances our response 
capabilities. Tasers are a vital, non-lethal tool that help officers manage high-risk encounters safely and 
effectively. As the manufacturer recommends a five-year life cycle, this renewal ensures our equipment remains 
reliable, compliant, and cost-effective throughout its intended operational life. 
Approval of this agreement will: 
•
Ensure full compliance with state mandates,
•
Support both officer and public safety,
•
Maintain flat budgeted costs through FY2030,
•
And secure long-term pricing stability.
Thank you for your review and consideration of this request. Please let me know if you have any questions or 
require additional information. 
Respe
v 
Matthew Crisafulli, Sheriff 
Worcester County Sheriffs Office

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Nicholas W. Rice, Procurement Officer 
DATE: 
August 5, 2025 
RE: 
Recommendation to Award – New Camera System 
Administration is recommending the County award the new countywide camera system replacement project to 
Absolute Security Group. Proposals were due and opened on Thursday, April 3, 2025. Seven proposals were 
received. I have attached the proposal tabulation to this memo. An evaluation team consisting of five members 
reviewed each proposal individually prior to an overall group average being established. All five members agree 
that the proposal received by Absolute Security Group represents the best value to Worcester County. The total 
contract amount is $997,500. 
We are requesting the replacement of the current surveillance system due to critical reliability issues. The system 
is obsolete, unsupported, lacks security updates, has maxed out its storage, and cannot be expanded or integrated 
with modern technology. A new system is essential for secure, scalable, and effective surveillance. 
Funding in the amount of $2,500,000 was approved in the FY25 operating budget under account 
100.1090.070.9010.040 - Capital Equipment IT Equipment. Due to required departmental collaboration to define 
the scope of work, the project was not completed in FY25. We are requesting that these funds be encumbered for 
use in FY26. 
Should you have any questions, please feel free to contact me. 
Worcester County New Camera System 
Thursday, April 3, 2025 at 2:30pm 
Request for Proposals Tabulation Sheet 
Respondent's Name(s): 
Absolute Security Group 
ARK Systems 
BizTech Fusion LLC 
CES Integrated Solutions 
LayerOne Technology Solutions Corp. 
Mid Point 
Johnson Controls Security Solutions 
ITEM 22
22 - 1

<!-- page 168 -->
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Nicholas W. Rice, Procurement Officer 
DATE: 
August 5, 2025 
RE: 
Request to Purchase – Fortinet Servers 
The IT Department is requesting approval to purchase networking equipment essential for supporting the 
infrastructure of the county’s video camera upgrade project. The proposed network switches will connect directly 
to our Fortinet Firewalls, ensuring optimal connectivity and seamless security integration. Our Fortinet support and 
licensing contracts are currently maintained through Verinext. The total cost of the purchase is $104,885.26, based 
on pricing available through the vendor’s contract with Omnia Partners. The existing switches used for the previous 
camera system are unmanaged and must be replaced due to the associated security risks. 
Funding in the amount of $2,500,000 was approved in the FY25 operative operating budget under account 
100.1090.070.9010.040 - Capital Equipment IT Equipment. Due to required departmental collaboration to define 
the scope of work, the project was not completed in FY25. We are requesting that these funds be encumbered for 
use in FY26. 
Omnia Partners is a national group purchasing organization that enables public sector entities to access 
competitively solicited contracts and volume discounts from vetted suppliers. By leveraging cooperative purchasing 
through Omnia, the county can streamline procurement, ensure compliance, and realize significant cost savings 
across a wide range of goods and services. 
Should you have any questions, please feel free to contact me.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Weston S. Young, P.E. Chief Administrative Officer 
Candace Savage, CGFM Deputy Chief Administrative Officer 
FROM: 
Dallas Baker, Jr., P.E., Director 
DATE: 
June 23, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Mulberry Road Speed Study  
Public Works is requesting Commissioner approval to reduce the speed on Mulberry Road 
from 50 mph to 30 mph. Currently, Mulberry Road is not posted; therefore, a speed limit of 50 mph 
governs. A concerned citizen reached out regarding speeding concerns on Mulberry Road, in 
response Roads Division conducted a speed study which yielded the following results: 
•
Number of Vehicles:  135
•
Average Speed:  26.0 mph
•
85th Percentile:  32 mph
•
Vehicles >50 mph: 0 or 0%
The study was conducted on July 1, 2025, thru July 8, 2025. A copy of the study is attached. 
After investigating this request and the area involved, Public Works recommends this road 
be posted at 30 mph.  This posting would allow the Sheriff’s Department to provide enforcement 
of the speed limit sign to control any speeding related issues.   
Please let me know if there are any questions. 
Attachment 
cc:  
Chris Clasing 
Kevin Lynch

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Weston Young P.E., Chief Administrative Officer 
Candace Savage, CGFM, Deputy Chief Administrative Officer 
FROM: 
Dallas Baker Jr., P.E., Director 
DATE: 
July 29, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Southern MD 589 Regional Water & Sewer Plan 
Public Works is requesting Commissioner approval and endorsement of the attached study 
from GMB entitled “MD 589 Water & Sewer Service Extension Evaluation” dated June 16, 2025. 
The goal of the study was to determine the sizes of the water mains, sewer mains, and number of 
sewer lift stations needed to serve the MD 589 corridor south of Pennington Commons and north 
of US 50, with a specific focus of minimizing future operational and maintenance costs. The study 
divided the corridor into two sub-sections, with Turville Creek being the dividing line. Each sub-
area would have its own regional sewage lift station (8-foot & 6-foot diameter, both 25-feet deep) 
and served by a combination of 6-inch & 8-inch sewer force mains, 8-inch & 10-inch gravity sewer 
mains, and 8-inch & 10-inch water mains. The lift stations have some potential for scaling their 
initial size by using smaller pumps and reduced generator requirements than those needed at full 
build out. Specific sizes and locations can be found in the attached study. The study did not 
evaluate any improvements to the existing downstream infrastructure which would be needed to 
support future projects in the subject corridor. 
To be clear, this plan is not a guarantee that any of the infrastructure will be built. The 
purpose is to provide an approved framework for developers to understand County requirements 
if development occurs in this area and determine their own costs when evaluating a potential 
project. An approved plan makes the expectations known upfront for everyone and helps the 
County reduce the cost of maintaining the system by reducing the number of sewage lift stations 
and ensuring pipes are adequately sized. 
Please let me know if there are any questions. 
Attachments 
CC: 
Bob Mitchell 
Jen Keener 
Quinn Dittrich 
Chris Clasing 
Tony Fascelli

Attn: 
Mr. Tony Fascelli 
Water & Wastewater Superintendent 
Re: 
MD 589 Water & Sewer Service Extension Evaluation 
Worcester County, MD 
Dear Mr. Fascelli: 
GMB is pleased to present our findings regarding the extension of municipal water and 
sewer to serve the MD 589 corridor.   Worcester County has seen an increased interest 
in development along MD 589 from the existing Pennington Commons development 
south to the Route 50 intersection.  It is anticipated sewer service to this area would come 
from Ocean Pines. The evaluation looked at two areas.  The first area (North Turville 
Creek) will include parcels that border MD 589 from the Pennington Commons 
development south to Turville Creek.  The second area (South Turville Creek) will include 
parcels south of Turville Creek to the intersection of Route 50.  Refer to EX-1: Parcel & 
Future EDU Map for details on each area boundary and associated future EDU’s 
included in the evaluation. 
GMB’s scope of work included developing concept layouts for gravity sewer with regional 
pump station and force main layouts, and a concept layout of water mains for each area 
discussed above.  The concept layout includes preliminary location, size and capacity 
parameters based on the EDU projections.  Based on topography and aerial data 
available via GIS databases, both service boundaries can be served by gravity sewer 
with one pump station (PS) associated with each area.  Detailed layouts of the gravity 
sewer and profiles are attached for reference.  Sheet C1.0 – Utility Key Sheet provides 
an overall layout of both sewer and water utilities.  Detailed preliminary design criteria 
have been compiled as an attachment to this letter, but some key parameters are noted 
below.      
Utility layouts are conceptual only.  No field survey or review of legal rights-of-ways 
(ROW) were completed under this evaluation. 
Area 1: North Turville Creek 

Total EDUs = 536 for an average daily flow of 134,000 gpd.

All parcels in boundary served by gravity.

One (1) proposed PS to collect sewer and pump wastewater to the existing Ocean
Pines PS-T.  (No evaluation of Ocean Pines sewer or downstream PS-T
completed at this time).
ITEM 25
25 - 2

<!-- page 184 -->
Mr. Tony Fascelli 
MD 589 W&S Service Evaluation 
June 16, 2025 
Page 2 

PS capacity is sized to handle flow from both Area 1 and Area 2.
o
Peak Flow = 625 gpm
o
Wet well = 8-foot diameter, 25-feet depth.
o
Force main = 8 inch for estimated velocity of 4 feet per second.
Area 2: South Turville Creek 

Total EDUs = 363 for an average daily flow of 90,750 gpd.

All parcels in boundary, with the exception of one, can be served by gravity.
o
Parcel 299 is located on Gray’s Corner Rd and cannot be served by
gravity due to existing drainage channel toward Mud Creek.  Per
Worcester County this parcel is not eligible for sewer service from an
expanded service area.  No EDUs associated with Parcel 299.
o
Parcels located along Gray’s Corner Rd., east of Route 50, not served by
gravity sewer at this time due to existing private agreement with Ocean
Down’s.

One (1) proposed PS to collect sewer and pump wastewater to Area 1 sewer.
o
Peak Flow = 250 gpm
o
Wet well = 6-foot diameter, 25-feet depth.
o
Force main = 6 inch for estimated velocity of 2.8 feet per second.
o
Required directional drill of force main under Turville Creek.
The design of each pump station shall follow MDE regulations inclusive of Design 
Guidelines For Wastewater Facilities dated 2016 requirements in addition to Worcester 
County DPW standard PS requirements.  The location of both PS facilities is in critical 
areas and within three (3) miles of shellfish water.  MDE emergency PS regulations 
require installation of a stationary generator in addition to one of the following, 1) third 
independent power source via diesel bypass pump system or 2nd standby generator or 
2) two (2) hour storage capacity at average daily flow.
It should be noted that GMB’s initial step in evaluating the gravity sewer options was to 
extend the existing sewer in Pennington Commons and/or utilize any remaining capacity 
available in the Pennington Commons pump station.   Note due to the shallow depth and 
diameter of this PS, any expansion is impractical and with the large proposed future 
service areas expanding this existing PS is not cost effective.   
Infrastructure for each area can potentially be phased based on the interest and location 
of development in the immediate future.  Several phasing parameters that could be 
considered further by Worcester County DPW include: 

Install gravity sewer to serve only parcel(s) being developed at this time.  Note
depths should be maintained for future connection.

Emergency MDE PS regulations (3rd power source or 2-hour storage) are
applicable only if flow capacity exceeds 150 gpm or 200 EDUs.  Consideration
to phasing this additional requirement once capacity exceeds the MDE limits.

Install smaller Hp pumps initially to serve existing and current planned EDU’s
only.  (Wet well structure and electrical service shall be installed for future
capacity).
ITEM 25
25 - 3

<!-- page 185 -->
Mr. Tony Fascelli 
MD 589 W&S Service Evaluation 
June 16, 2025 
Page 3 
The proposed water main layout includes extending a 10-inch main from Pennington 
Commons to parcels along MD 589 as needed.  Looping of the proposed main to existing 
mains should be considered where possible.   
We appreciate the opportunity to complete this evaluation for planning purposes of the 
future MD 589 service area.  Please don’t hesitate to contact us with any questions or to 
discuss this evaluation in more detail.   
Sincerely, 
Katherine J. McAllister, P.E. 
Sr. Project Manager/ Vice President

EDU Table Notes:
1. Commercial properties at 1,300 gpd/acre and took off 20% area for environmental/SWM constraints and divided by 300gpd/EDU.
2. A2 areas at average residential density of 5 units/ac between residential zoning R2 to R4, assuming rezoning.
3. Residential calculation was acreage x 4 and take off 20% for env./SWM constraints to give # of residential EDUs.
4. Existing septic got 1 EDU for existing except where larger systems for commercial use.
6. Two large agricultural parcels above Gum Point (203, 105), increased area for env/SWM and used .6 as multiplier for acreage.
7. Tax Map 21, Parcel 299 is Agricultural land use component in the Comp Plan.  Not eligible to receive sewer service from an expanded planning area.
9. Tax Map 21, Parcels 158, 264, 157, 208, & 96 account for 50 EDUs collectively.  Parcel 264 parcel proposing an indoor sports building for wrestling and soccer.
10. Parcel 65: 3 EDUs - Frank Lynch Survey office & farm to the west.
11. Parcel 118: 1 EDU – Can only have one house unless have a growth allocation as RCA is more than 50% of the land. 
5. The connected property on Tax Map 21, Parcel 66, Lot 2 (AGH  21-66 2) will have another 12 EDUs at buildout.  Existing EDUs connectd to Ocean Pines sewer 
service area by private grinder pump station.  Future infrastrucutre should include connection of this parcel and abandon grinder pump station. 
8. Tax Map 21, Parcel 161 is the Lakehaven MHC with a community wastewater system.   EDUs accounted for as future due to concerns with septic failure and there 
may come a time when the park owners request County sewer service inclusion.

MD 589 Water & Sewer Evaluation 
Gravity Sewer Analysis – Detailed Design Criteria & Notes 
GMB Project No. 250092 
Date: 6/13/2025 
 
Area 1: North Turville Creek (Gum Point Road to Pennington) 
1. Gravity Sewer Coverage: 
a. 22 Parcels totaling 224.65 acres (See Table) 
b. Gum Point Rd parcels are not part of this Study Area 
2. Potential EDUs: 
a. Total = 536  
3. Sewer Design Analysis: 
a. Trunk lines (10” SDR-35 or SDR-26 PVC at minimum 0.28% slope) 
i. 10” capacity = 821 EDUs 
ii. MD 589 R.O.W. or dedicated easement along east side 
iii. Assume Parcel 0066, Lot B & 1 discharge directly into PS-1 (allows 
sewer to be 10” based on capacity).  
b. Branch lines (8” SDR-35 PVC at minimum 0.40% slope) 
i. 8” capacity = 536 EDUs 
ii. Taylorville Lane access to Parcels 0105, 0203, and 0217; 
iii. Adkins Road spur 
4. Manholes: 
a. Invert In = Invert out 
b. Total = 26 manholes 
5. Pump Station (PS-1) Analysis: 
a. Proposed Location: Parcel 0066 with access from Atlantic General Pediatrics 
entrance/service road. 
b. Includes: Pump Station wet well with mechanical ventilation, valve vault, 
bypass pumping connection, generator, telemetry alarm and control panel 
within fenced area. 
c. 3rd power source – per MDE Emergency PS regs. 
d. Force main leaves Parcel 0066 and runs north along MD 589 R.O.W. then 
turns into Ocean Pines-Nottingham Lane to Royal Okas Dr to PS-T.  The 
County has 15-foot easement between all parcels.    
6. Pump Station (PS-1) Design: See detailed design data sheet. 
a. Max depth 25-feet. 
b. Pump submergence min = 2 feet assumed 
c. Est. pump Hp (full build out): 15 HP, 3 ph, 240V

1. Gravity Sewer Coverage:

b. Includes 120 EDUs for future Lake Haven MHC if the decision is made to

3. Sewer Design Analysis:

gravity.   
b. Branch lines (8” SDR-35 PVC at minimum 0.40% slope) 
i. 8” capacity = 536 EDUs 
ii. Parcel connection branch lines  
4. Manholes: 
a. Invert In = Invert Out 
b. Total = 20 
5. Pump Station (PS-2) Analysis: 
a. Proposed Location: Ocean Down’s parcel near MD 589 ROW. 
b. Includes: Pump Station wet well with mechanical ventilation, valve vault, 
bypass pumping connection, generator, telemetry alarm, and control panel 
within fenced area. 
c. 3rd power source – per MDE Emergency PS regs.

July 16, 2025 
To: Worcester County Commissioners 
From: Administration 
RE: Local Government Justice Reinvestment Commission 
****************************************************************************** 
Assistant Warden Shytina Drummond has been selected to serve on the Worcester County Seat 
for the Local Government Justice Reinvestment Commission. 
The Justice Reinvestment Act, signed into law in 2016, was developed to reduce the state’s 
prison population and corrections spending by investing in strategies that decrease crime and 
reduce recidivism.  
“As a result of the Justice Reinvestment Act, also known as Chapter 515, a local Government 
Justice Reinvestment Commission was established. This commission provides guidance to the 
Justice Reinvestment Oversight Board on the local effects of the Act, offers recommendations 
for specific grants to local governments, and develops performance metrics to evaluate the 
effectiveness of those grants.” 
Shytina is seeking an official letter of support and appointment from the Worcester County 
Commissioners, nominating her as the representative for Worcester County on this Commission. 
Thank you for your consideration.

experience needed to effectively serve on the Local Government Reinvestment Commission. Please 
contact Worcester County Administration if further information is needed.

 County Commissioners

Advisory

<!-- page 234 -->
Worcester County Board Of License Commissioners
7/22/2025
Board Description:
Regulates retail sale of alcoholic beverages in Worcester County.
Board Information
Comm. No.
0
Statutory Language
There is a Board of License Commissioners for Worcester County. The Governor shall appoint three members to the Board, subject to the advice and consent of the Senate. Each member of the Board shall be: a 
resident and voter of the county; and an individual of high character and integrity and of recognized business capacity. The term of a member is 4 years. The terms of the members are staggered as required by the 
terms provided for members of the Board on July 1, 2016. The Governor shall appoint an eligible individual to fill a vacancy during the remainder of the term of office of the individual originally appointed in accordance 
with subsection (a) of this section. A member who is appointed after a term has begun serves only for the remainder of the term and until a successor is appointed and qualifies. The Governor may remove a member 
for misconduct in office, incompetence, or willful neglect of duty. The Governor shall give a member who is charged a copy of the charges against the member and, with at least 10 days’ notice, an opportunity to be 
heard publicly in person or by counsel. If a member is removed, the Governor shall file with the Office of the Secretary of State a statement of charges against the member and the Governor’s findings on the charges. 
In addition to the regular members of the Board, the Governor shall appoint a substitute Board member. The term of the substitute member is 4 years. The substitute member: shall serve on the Board in the absence 
of a quorum of the regular members due to illness or conflict of interest; and has all of the powers and duties of a regular member when serving on the Board. In making the appointments, the Governor shall designate 
a chair from among the members of the Board. The chair of the Board shall receive a salary of not less than $2,500 annually, as determined by the County Commissioners. Each other regular member of the Board 
shall receive a salary of not less than $2,100 annually, as determined by the County Commissioners. The substitute member of the Board shall receive compensation that the County Commissioners determine for 
services when acting on the Board. The Board of County Commissioners shall pay the salaries and expenses of the Board of License Commissioners.

NOTICE  
OF  
PROPOSED CHANGE IN ZONING 
 
EAST SIDE OF RACETRACK ROAD 
AT TAYLORVILLE LANE, BERLIN 
THIRD TAX DISTRICT 
WORCESTER COUNTY, MARYLAND 
 
Pursuant to Section 1-113 of the Worcester County Zoning Ordinance, Rezoning Case No. 447 has been filed by 
Hugh Cropper on behalf of Maryland Medical Owners II, LLC and Maryland Medical Owners III, LLC, property 
owners, for an amendment to the Official Zoning Maps to change approximately 22.86 acres of land located on 
the east side of MD Route 589 (Racetrack Road) at Taylorville Lane, Berlin, in the Third Tax District of 
Worcester County, Maryland, from C-2 General Commercial District to R-3 Multi-family Residential District. 
The Planning Commission has given a favorable recommendation with condition to the rezoning application. 
 
Pursuant to Sections 1-113 and 1-114 of the Worcester County Zoning Ordinance, the County Commissioners 
will hold a  
PUBLIC HEARING 
on 
TUESDAY, AUGUST 5, 2025 
AT 10:30 AM 
IN THE COUNTY COMMISSIONERS’ MEETING ROOM 
WORCESTER COUNTY GOVERNMENT CENTER – ROOM 1101 
ONE WEST MARKET STREET 
SNOW HILL, MARYLAND 21863 
 
At said public hearing the County Commissioners will consider the rezoning application, the staff file on 
Rezoning Case No. 447 and the recommendation of the Planning Commission, any proposed restrictions on the 
rezoning, other appropriate restrictions, conditions or limitations as may be deemed by them to be appropriate to 
preserve, improve, or protect the general character and design of the lands and improvements being zoned or 
rezoned or of the surrounding or adjacent lands and improvements, and the advisability of reserving the power 
and authority to approve or disapprove the design of buildings, construction, landscaping or other improvements, 
alterations and changes made or to be made on the subject land or lands to assure conformity with the intent and 
purpose of applicable State laws and regulations and the County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
Maps of the petitioned area, the staff file on Rezoning Case No. 447 and the Planning Commission’s 
recommendation, which will be entered into record at the public hearing, are on file and available to view
